# IvritSuite Improvement Log

The memory of the continuous-improvement loop. **Read this first every session.** One concern per
iteration, one commit per iteration. Prioritization: P1 (data loss/security/broken core/export
corruption) > P2 (silently wrong output/undo holes/a11y blockers) > P3 (perf/dead UI/confusing
copy/consistency) > P4 (polish). Tie-breakers: (1) affects teachers' saved work, (2) affects the
printed/exported artifact a student receives, (3) dual-audience (Hebrew + secular) wins, (4) smallest diff.

## Candidates (prioritized, top = next)

_(**S190 Pass L** — SEO & discoverability, **5th run**, taken on the S189 pointer's explicit instruction not to defer it a second time. The pointer's method worked exactly as designed: the **byte check ran FIRST** and cleared the copy dimensions in one command (`git diff -U0 1dcd2d7..HEAD -- ':(top)*.html'` → **0 changed lines** matching `<title>`/`name="description"`/`rel="canonical"`/`og:`/`twitter:`/`ld+json`, across **3,538 insertions in 7 root HTML files**), freeing the whole pass to converge on the dimensions that CAN have moved. **Structurally CLEAN for the 5th consecutive run:** canonicals 11/11 with the bare-root homepage; 404/i18n-test noindex + sitemap-absent (**and 404's canonical is still gone — the S174 finding fixed in S175 has not regressed**); sitemap↔canonical **exact 11/11 both directions**; 11/11 `<lastmod>` present; robots.txt Sitemap-directive intent correct with no blanket disallow; **35 JSON-LD blocks parse**; both og/twitter images (`og-card.png`, `og-fontmaker.png`) resolve on disk; titles+descriptions unique with sane lengths (titles 27–61, descriptions 92–164); **exactly one `<h1>` on all 13 pages**; **0 `<img>` without `alt`**; and the **crawl-graph 11/11 reachable from the homepage** (10 direct links, 0 pageerror across the crawl, and the crawl reached nothing outside the indexable set).
**The one dimension a byte check cannot clear produced all 3 findings — "do the claims still match what the tool does".** The copy is byte-identical; the UI beneath it moved 3,538 lines. All three were gate-2 approved and **all three shipped this session** (3237da8): FM's HowTo naming 3 of the wizard's 4 sources (Drawing mode absent) under a step still called "Upload your letters"; the dashboard's Schedule Sync answer describing "a preset and an end time to each period" — precisely the single-day format the tool now calls "your existing schedule" and offers to migrate into the weekly grid; and the dictionary's filter list omitting Browse by Theme.
**The scope discovery that mattered:** each JSON-LD claim has a **VISIBLE FAQ twin** on the same page, and every twin carried the *identical* stale wording in **both languages** — so the structured data was never diverging from the page; the page had fallen behind the tool. Fixing only the JSON-LD would have manufactured a fresh JSON-LD↔visible mismatch, worse than either, so both halves moved together (3 JSON-LD claims + 3 localized EN/HE strings, worded to agree).
**⚑ This REVISES a convention S142's L run ratified.** That run saw the same shape and wrote "newer features like dictionary themes / torah copy simply aren't enumerated, nothing claimed is stale" — i.e. non-enumeration is not staleness. S190 narrows it: a **closed enumeration that asserts the complete set** ("one per image, a full sheet, or an existing font file") IS stale once the set grows, and a description of *how a feature works* that no longer describes it (the dashboard) was never non-enumeration at all. Merely incomplete prose still isn't a hit. The dictionary item is the one S142 explicitly declined; it shipped this time because the maintainer chose all three at gate 2.
**Two would-be findings were KILLED by grounding before either was logged.** (1) og:title/og:description differ from twitter:title/twitter:description on **9 of 11 pages** — **not drift**: every tool page deliberately ships a shorter, brand-suffixed X variant (`Hebrew Flash Cards | IvritSuite`), with 9/11 twitter descriptions shorter than their og twin by design. My equality assertion was stricter than the property the site guarantees and failed 9 correct pages; the real contract (og set complete, `og:url === canonical`, `twitter:image === og:image`, `twitter:card` large) passes 11/11. (2) `404.html` reported a missing canonical as a FAIL — **already fixed in S175** (1c567d4); my diff window simply began after that commit. Both are now regression guards instead of findings.)_

_(**S189 Pass F** — cross-tool consistency, 16th run. Affordance: **busy / loading feedback during a slow operation** — never swept in 15 prior F runs (destructive resets S176, transient toasts S111/S155, share links S133, fullscreen + dark toggles S123, shared-block byte-identity S144/S154, font pickers S80/S100, tour pulses S90, empty states S71). Contract fixed BEFORE measuring, five dimensions: **PRESENT** (visible in-flight text) · **ANNOUNCED** (in a live region) · **CLEARS-OK** · **CLEARS-FAIL** (replaced by an error, never left as "Loading…") · **GUARDED** (no stacked re-fire). Method: each tool's own primary slow resource stalled 2.6–3.0 s and, separately, 502'd; sampled mid-flight and after settle; local HTTP, `serviceWorkers:'block'`.
**Five of seven tools PASS on every dimension:** torah_trainer (`#ttStatus` holds "Loading <label>…" for the whole 2.6 s window, `role=status aria-live=polite`, error line on 502, `_readingLoading`/`_fetchSeq` re-entrancy guard), trope_tutor (`#tuStatus`), font_maker (the most elaborate in the suite — engine badge + phased export-button label + sticky `#statusMsg` + a localized `askModal` on failure), dictionary (visible download progress in KB and %), dashboard (a per-widget line that correctly becomes "Weather unavailable"). **Two laggards; all four findings fixed this session** — see Done.
**Two candidate findings were KILLED by grounding before either was logged.** (1) font_maker's `#statusMsg` appeared to be stuck at "Loading font engine (one-time ~8 MB)…" permanently after a failure — **REFUTED at the reachable path**: all three foreground callers (`exportFont`, `generatePreviewPDF`, `offerSaveToMyFonts`) do `status('')` and then raise a localized error modal, and the background prefetch writes no status at all; my probe had called `ensurePyodide()` with no caller. (2) torah_trainer appeared to show **no** busy text — **a harness fault**: a `waitForFunction` on `#ttParshaSelect option` (that select has no direct `option` children) burned the entire in-flight window before the first sample.
**Informational, deliberately NOT logged as a convergence:** `aria-busy` appears in **0 of 9** files suite-wide. F's own rule treats a uniform absence as enhancement-to-all rather than a laggard, and every carrier already announces through `role="status"`, so `aria-busy` would add nothing.)_

_(**S191 Pass C** — accessibility, one tool: **`torah_trainer.html`**, the S190 pointer's strongly-favored target and its 4th dedicated C (S18 → S72 → S146 → S191). The point of the pick was the three interactive surfaces added since S146 that had never had a dedicated audit — the S190 sticky bulk dock, the S188 type-a-range verse picker, the copy bar — and **the pick paid: 2 of the 3 findings that shipped were defects in the bulk dock itself, one of them a P2.** Structurally the page remains very strong: 124 visible controls with 0 naming defects in either language, 64-stop tab order in a sensible region sequence, a clean drawer focus-trap/Escape/restore contract, 20/20 visible focus rings in both themes, 0 pageerrors everywhere.)_
**The method lesson of the run: the two loudest automated signals were both wrong, and both would have been logged by a less suspicious pass.** (1) "35 of 64 tab stops have no focus indicator" — Chromium computes its own UA focus ring as `outline: <color> auto 0px`, so **`getComputedStyle().outlineWidth` cannot detect a focus ring at all**; a screenshot pixel-diff refuted it 20/20 in both themes. Any future C run that reads styles for this is measuring nothing. (2) "6 tab stops are invisible (0×0)" — those are the toggle-switch `<input>`s, deliberately hidden behind their `.slider`, which the existing `input:focus-visible + .slider` rule rings correctly. **A third near-miss on the verification side:** the fix-verification harness reported the gold **Copy All** button at 1:1 because an opaque `rgb()` parses to 3 components, so an `alpha > 0` guard is `false` for it and every solid-background element gets measured against its ancestor's plate instead of its own fill. The guard must be `alpha === undefined || alpha > 0`.
**And the injection lesson, which cost the first three probe runs:** `currentVerses` is a top-level `let` in a non-module inline script, so it lives in the global **lexical** environment and is **not** a `window` property. `window.currentVerses = …` silently creates a shadowed twin — the harness reported the reading as rendered while `.tt-word` count was 0. A **bare assignment** is the only way in, and `injectReading` now throws on a 0-word receipt rather than proceeding.

_(**S184 Pass D** — performance, `torah_trainer.html`, its first dedicated D since **S66, 118 sessions ago**. The pass ran as a 5-surface workflow that was KILLED mid-run when the session was suspended: 2 surfaces (boot/render, toggle-churn) reported, 3 (copy+bulk-select scaling, karaoke runtime, session hygiene/leaks) never ran, and the adversarial-verify phase never ran at all. The two headline findings below were therefore re-measured by hand in two separate process runs before being logged — which corrected one of them. **The three unprofiled surfaces are the reason D should be run again soon; see the rotation note.**)_

- [ ] P3 | torah_trainer.html | **Every transliteration-enabled cold load renders the whole parsha TWICE, and the discarded first pass paints a false "Transliteration unavailable" chip.** `init()` fires `HebrewTransliterator.ensureLoaded()` and then `fetchAndRender()` without waiting, so the first `renderText()` runs while the lazily-imported esm.sh ESM is still in flight: `_translitDead` is true, the reading is built with no translit rows AND a `tt-notice-chip` saying transliteration is unavailable; `wireTranslit`'s `translitlibloaded` handler then rebuilds the entire reading. Measured (S184 Pass D, `#ttReading.innerHTML` setter instrumented, 146 verses, everything on, TWO separate process runs): exactly 2 reading writes both runs — write 1 at t=152/166 ms, 403 KB, 0 translit rows, chip present; write 2 at t=286/309 ms, 498 KB, 146 translit rows, chip gone; gap between paints 134/143 ms at cpu 1 (the profiling agent measured the same shape at 179–412 ms, and 1.4–1.6 s at cpu 4). The wasted pass IS a full `renderText`, so it scales linearly with the reading (5.3/13.4/35.2/75.2 ms at 20/60/146/300 verses). Everything-off loads render exactly ONCE, so this is specific to the translit path. Fix = when `settings.showTranslit`, await `Promise.race([ensureLoaded(), timeout(~2s)])` before the first `renderText`, so the common case renders once and a genuinely blocked CDN still falls through to today's dead-path render. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [ ] P3 | torah_trainer.html | **A colour-picker drag, a font-size drag, or the dark toggle restyles the entire reading, so the live preview that makes those controls usable runs at ~10–24 fps on a full parsha.** The JS is already right — S60/S66's var-swap design holds (re-verified S184: `toggleDark` produces **0** `#ttReading` mutations and ~0.3 ms of JS) — the cost is browser restyle over 4,269 `.nik` spans + 1,558 trope-classed words. **PARTIAL, and the correction matters:** the profiling agent reported `toggleDark` at a steady 212–232 ms, but two independent re-measurements gave medians of **352 ms and 161 ms** (samples 148–444 ms) — the baseline is far noisier than one run suggests, so do not quote a single figure. What IS stable across every run is the remedy: adding `#ttReading .tt-verse { content-visibility: auto; contain-intrinsic-size: auto 140px; }` inside `@media screen` measured **90 ms in both runs** (samples 82–98), i.e. a ~2–4× improvement, not the ~7× a single baseline implied. Agent-measured drag frames at 146 verses, cpu 1: font-size slider 95–101 ms/frame (~10 fps), trope picker 49–59 ms (~19 fps), nikkud picker 41–43 ms (~24 fps); ~4–6× worse at cpu 4. Scales linearly with verse count. Fix is one screen-only CSS rule — but it changes scroll/print behaviour of off-screen verses, so verify the print pipeline and the karaoke auto-scroll before landing it. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [ ] P4 | torah_trainer.html | **`renderLoopState()` rewrites the label and aria-label of every verse's Loop button on every render even when no loop is running — 97% of all mutation records a render produces.** Measured (S184 Pass D, mutation-record attribution, 146 verses, cpu 1): a plain `renderText()` emits 301 records, of which 292 are `childList|BUTTON.tt-loop` (146) + `attributes|BUTTON.tt-loop[aria-label]` (146); the reading rebuild itself accounts for ~9. Corroborated by switching to `biblepage`, which emits no Loop buttons: exactly 9 records. Isolated cost with `loopState.active === false`: 0.5/1.1/2.6/6.1 ms at 20/60/146/300 verses (11.6 ms at 146 / cpu 4), re-dirtying DOM the `innerHTML` write three statements earlier had just built with those exact labels. `syncBulkClasses()` is the same shape at 0.4 ms. Fix = early-return both when there is nothing to do (`!loopState.active` and no `.tt-looping` present; `ttBulkSelected.size === 0` and no `.tt-bulk-selected`), and guard `clearLoop()`'s call with its existing `was` flag. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [ ] P4 | torah_trainer.html | **The `colorCodeTrope` master toggle forces a full reading rebuild that a body-class swap could avoid — the gating class already exists.** Every trope CSS rule is already gated on `body.trope-on` (`:where(body.trope-on…)` and siblings, plus the print block and `.trope-underline-fallback`), and `applyTropeColors()` already flips that class, all 12 CSS vars and the legend. But `tokenizeHebrew` guards emission of `trope-<fam>` on `settings.colorCodeTrope`, so the toggle takes a rebuild instead. Measured (S184 Pass D, 146 verses, medians of 5): JS 40–42 ms, frame 137–140 ms, longtask 95–98, 301 mutation records each way at cpu 1; ~730 ms frame at cpu 4. Fix = emit the classes unconditionally (the family computation already runs off the ORIGINAL text and is cheap) and change the `#optColorCodeTrope` handler from `renderText()` to `applyTropeColors()`. P4 because it is a once-or-twice-per-session control. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D
- [ ] P4 (informational — comment correction only) | torah_trainer.html | **`renderText`'s `_translitDead` comment claims `.every()` short-circuits "so when the library is present this costs exactly one extra call" — true on the LIVE path, false on the dead one.** When the library is absent (offline, blocked esm.sh, or the pre-load window of the double-render finding above) `every()` returns true, which can only happen after visiting all N verses, each paying `replaceTetragrammaton(stripTeamim(...))`. Measured at ~0.003 ms/verse — ~1 ms at a full parsha, i.e. below the noise floor. No performance change warranted; worth one comment correction if anyone edits that line. | found: 2026-08-06, S184 Pass D


- [ ] P4 (informational — logged, not changed) | torah_trainer.html | **`textCache` grows one full parsha per distinct reading and is never evicted.** Measured (S185 Pass D, session-hygiene surface, two independent process runs): 12 successive parsha loads → `textCache.size` 12, one entry per `ref|version` key, each holding that reading's complete verse array (146 verses × Hebrew + English strings for a full parsha). It is **bounded by the corpus**, not unbounded — 54 parshiyot × the scopes/translation versions a teacher actually opens — and everything else about repeated loading is provably clean (DOM nodes, listeners and live elements all FLAT across 12 loads). So this is a note for whoever next profiles memory on this page, not a defect: the ceiling is small and the cache is what makes re-picking a parsha instant. | found: 2026-08-06, S185 Pass D

- [x] ✅ DONE S186, cd36f76 (gate-2 approved as drafted at the S186 batch; 2 new shared.fonts.* keys; AX-verified per-row names EN+HE on both carriers) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html + index.html | **The My Fonts ⬇/🗑 buttons name the ACTION but not the ROW.** S183 (03f64a4) fixed the symbol-only names, so a screen reader no longer hears "🗑" — but ten saved fonts now give ten identical "Remove from My Fonts" with nothing identifying which font each row is. Mechanically trivial (the font name is in scope at both build sites); it needs an approved `{name}` key per carrier, e.g. "Remove {name} from My Fonts" / "Download {name}". `aria-describedby` pointing at the row's name span is a zero-copy alternative, but descriptions are announced late and some readers suppress them. | found: 2026-08-02, S183

_(**S179 Pass A** added the block below — 31 findings, 23 adversarially verified (0 refuted; 5 corrected to PARTIAL). The **four data-integrity P2s** are this session's gate-1-approved slate and are closed inline; everything under them is new open backlog.)_

- [x] ✅ DONE S179, 47818ef | hebrew_dictionary.html | **A damaged word-list store kills the Word Lists manager — dead button, data still in storage** (S179 Pass A, untrusted-shape-on-read). `wlReadStore()` validates only `typeof s.lists === 'object'` and passes every entry through untouched, so ONE bad list from an imported AllTools/`.ivrit` file (hand-editable text; `index.html wlMergeIntoStorage` copies per-list values verbatim) breaks three consumers that all read through it: `wlRenderManagerInto`, `wlOpenSaveModal`, `wlOpenAddToListModal` share the crash expression `(store.lists[b].updated || 0)`, and the manager adds a second unguarded `list.words.length`. **Measured (my own headless receipts, light/1280 + dark/800, store bytes intact every time):** `lists:{a:null}` → `wlOpenManager()` throws `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'name')`, manager NEVER OPENS; list with no `words` → throws `…undefined (reading 'length')`, never opens; `words:"לחם"` → opens, miscounts "3 words" (string length), then **View words** throws `words.forEach is not a function`; `words:[null,…]` → miscounts, View words throws `…null (reading 'word')`. Fix = normalize per-list shape at the `wlReadStore()` chokepoint, non-destructively (never rewrite storage on read) — both sibling consumers already do exactly this (`flash_cards.html` + `hebrew_blend_generator.html`: `Array.isArray(l.words) ? l.words : []`). | found: 2026-08-01, S179 Pass A
- [x] ✅ DONE S179, 391fc69 | classroom_dashboard.html | **A malformed saved schedule blanks the Schedule Builder AND is written back to storage** (S179 Pass A, untrusted-shape-on-read; verifier-CONFIRMED with its own repro). `renderScheduleValidation()`'s `const rows = (settings.currentSchedule || []).filter(e => e.preset || e.until)` assumes an array of objects; both feeders skip shape checks — `loadSavedSchedule` (`JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(savedSchedules[name]))`) and `loadSettingsFromStorage` (`ivritSafeAssign(settings, saved)`), unlike their siblings `normalizePanelLayout` / `migrateRostersInSettings`, which DO validate. Measured: `currentSchedule:[null]` → init pageerror `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'preset')`; `"str"` and `{}` → `…filter is not a function`. Fix = validate/normalize `currentSchedule` on read, mirroring the two sibling normalizers already in the file. | found: 2026-08-01, S179 Pass A
- [x] ✅ DONE S179, baf68bc | index.html | **An AllTools import silently discards a student's Learning Path progress** (S179 Pass A, ivrit-gather-gap/merge asymmetry; verifier-CONFIRMED with a negative control). `mergeFlashCardProfiles(incoming, replace)` takes the whole profile only when the name is NEW (`if (!base.profiles[name]) { base.profiles[name] = src; return; }`); for an existing name it merges `results` only and never reads `src.ladder`, so the imported ladder — and with it the completion certificate the S177-era feature prints — is dropped. `flash_cards.html`'s sibling `mergeProfilesBlob` has the extra pass, with the comment "Only `results` is merged above, so an imported backup's ladder needs its own pass." → `mergeLadder(...)`. Fix = give index.html's merge the same ladder pass (the helper's semantics are already ledgered: per-level first-earn wins). | found: 2026-08-01, S179 Pass A
- [x] ✅ DONE S179, 407a94a | flash_cards.html | **The tool's own "Save to .ivrit file" button omits BOTH folder trees, in all three backup scopes** (S179 Pass A, ivrit-gather-gap; verifier-CONFIRMED and WIDER than first claimed). `flashBackupSave()` is a scope-aware save path that shadows the shared engine's `ivritSaveFile()`, and it builds its payload by hand: measured on the real button with real trees seeded, `scope=presets` → `["presets","liveState"]`, `scope=profiles`/`both` likewise carry no `presetFolders`/`profileFolders` — while `IVRIT_CFG.gather()` (unused by the button) DOES include both and `IVRIT_CFG.apply()` restores both. So a teacher's whole folder organisation is absent from the tool's own backup and silently flattens to root on restore. Fix = have the scope builder emit the matching folder tree(s) via `ftRead`, per scope. | found: 2026-08-01, S179 Pass A
- [x] ✅ DONE S183, 21e6f54 (all 4 sites; 0 new copy; census gen 16→14, fc 19→17, dash 11→10) | hebrew_blend_generator.html + flash_cards.html + classroom_dashboard.html | **Blocking `alert()` still guards four routine paths that the inline-validation rule already covers elsewhere in the SAME file** (S179 Pass A). _(**S180 Pass H** added the block below — `torah_trainer.html`'s first teacher walkthrough since **S77, 103 sessions ago**, run as 4 parallel lesson-prep scenarios (bar-mitzvah setup, printed class handout, copying verses into a worksheet, cold first-week setup) + adversarial verification. 3 of 4 scenarios reported (the handout agent died; its print path was covered by the top finding's own receipts). **The seed queue goes from empty — for the first time in six sessions — to 12.**)_

- [x] ✅ DONE S180, 1584f00 | torah_trainer.html | **"Hide nikkud" also deletes the maqaf, the sof pasuk and the paseq — welding words into nonsense on screen, in the clipboard, on the printed student handout, AND shifting the karaoke word indices** (S180 Pass H; **verified four ways with my own receipts, not an agent's**). `NIKKUD_RE = /[ְ-ׇ]/g` is U+05B0–U+05C7, which contains U+05BE maqaf, U+05C0 paseq, U+05C3 sof pasuk and U+05C6 nun hafukha — punctuation, not points. Its own comment says "nikkud only (vowels + dagesh + meteg)", and the sibling `TEAMIM_RE` (U+0591–U+05AF) correctly keeps all four, so this is a bug, not a design call. **Measured:** `stripNikkud("וַיֹּ֥אמֶר … וַֽיְהִי־אֽוֹר׃")` → `"וי֥אמר … ויהיאור"` — two words fused into one nonsense word and the verse-ending ׃ gone; reproduced by driving the real **Show nikkud** toggle, in `verseCopyText()`'s clipboard string, and — snapshotted **at print time** under `body.tt-handout-print` — on the **student handout** (`welded:true, maqaf:false, sofPasuk:false`). **The consequence the walkthrough missed:** `tokenizeHebrew` splits on `(\s+|־)` because PocketTorah counts each side of a maqaf pair as a separate sung word, and `data-twi` indexes the timings array directly — so hiding nikkud drops the sung-word count **27 → 24** (per-verse 7/14/6 → 7/12/5) and every word after the first maqaf highlights against the wrong audio timing. Fix = narrow the class to `[\u05B0-\u05BD\u05BF\u05C1\u05C2\u05C4\u05C5\u05C7]`, keeping the four punctuation codepoints; the `tokenizeHebrew` comment that documents the swallow then needs re-truing (keep the `tropeRealignFamilies` guard — it just stops firing). | found: 2026-08-02, S180 Pass H
- [x] ✅ DONE S180, b7b7b7a | **P3, corrected from P2 by verification** (found INDEPENDENTLY by 2 of 3 walkthroughs; the verifier reproduced the core with a stronger receipt and **refuted two details**) | torah_trainer.html | **Fullscreen — the mode the page sells as SmartBoard projection — ignores all three font-size sliders, and hides every control that could fix it.** `body.fullscreen .tt-heb, body.fullscreen .tt-cell.heb { font-size: 2rem }` beats `.tt-heb { font-size: var(--tt-heb-size) }` on specificity, so pressing the project button makes 2.6rem text **23% SMALLER** (41.6px → 32px) — and it ignores the slider in BOTH directions (0.9rem → 14.4px windowed → 32px fullscreen). **The control is dead with a lying readout:** driven live inside real native fullscreen, `#optHebSizeVal` reported 3.50rem then 0.90rem and `--tt-heb-size` updated both times while the render stayed pinned at 32px. No escape hatch: `body.fullscreen header{display:none}` leaves `#gearBtn` a 0×0 box with no keyboard path, so the teacher must leave fullscreen — where the change then won't apply. **Verifier CORRECTION 1 — "Page view honours the slider" is FALSE**, a measurement artifact (the reporter measured the wrapper `<p class="tt-bible-heb">`, not the `.tt-heb` spans that carry the words): all three layouts are overridden, and page view looks WORST because the 133px drop-cap and the slider-scaled verse superscripts stay put while the body text drops to 32px — so "add `.tt-bible-heb` to the rule" would make it worse. **CORRECTION 2 — scope understated:** translit is overridden in all three layouts too (32→18.4px), and the real layout inconsistency is on the ENGLISH row (`.tt-translation` is in the rule; plain `.tt-cell` and `.tt-bible-translation` are not). **Fix idiom already exists ~150 lines away:** `body.tt-handout-size-large{--tt-heb-size:2.2rem}` — set the body-level VAR, never `font-size` (its own comment explains the var beats `applyFontSizes`' documentElement inline var for every reading element). **P3 not P2:** the setting persists and is restored exactly on exit (measured), and nothing wrong is printed, copied or exported — but it is top-of-band, since the imposed 32px ceiling is too small to read across a room, which is the mode's entire job. Torah is the OUTLIER: the dashboard hardcodes fullscreen sizes too, but its elements have no per-element slider AND it keeps its zoom bar reachable plus ships an explicit ⚙️ Settings button in fullscreen. | found: 2026-08-02, S180 Pass H

- [x] ✅ DONE S185, 30fe852 | torah_trainer.html | **`checkTTSVoiceAvailability()` writes a hardcoded English string** although `torah.audio.tts_no_voice` exists, is translated, and has no call site. **FIXED S185 — and grounding found the naive fix would have shipped a raw key AND a listener leak.** The function runs at init, before `I18n.ready`, and `applyI18n` never re-ran it (the registered **pre-ready-i18n / never-re-rendered** class, 4th instance), so `I18n.t()` alone would have frozen `torah.audio.tts_no_voice` on screen in both languages. The readout therefore moved into a **named** `syncTTSVoiceStatus()` that `applyI18n` calls — named rather than the previous fresh-closure-per-call `check()`, because `speechSynthesis` survives every re-render, so re-running the old shape from `applyI18n` would have stacked a new `voiceschanged` listener on every language switch. Verified EN+HE × light/dark × 1280/800: real Hebrew on screen, and 5× `applyI18n` adds **0** listeners.

_(**S181 Pass G** added the block below — `torah_trainer.html`'s **first-ever dedicated G** (the pointer's named target; G was stalest at S169). Run as a 4-surface parallel workflow (student handout / side-by-side parallel print / interlinear+Page-view+chrome / ink fidelity+clipboard) over **real PDFs through Chromium's print pipeline at Letter AND A4**, page counts read from the PDF bytes. **~24 findings, 7 at P2.** Every finding is print-artifact — the rubric's tie-breaker #2. **The whole set is one tool, so the variety governor caps this session at 2 of them.**)_

- [x] ✅ DONE S181, ce1eab2 | torah_trainer.html | **A4 paper silently discards every Hebrew font-size choice — the handout's three presets AND the teacher's font slider** (S181 Pass G; found INDEPENDENTLY by 3 of 4 surfaces plus my own measurement, then adversarially CONFIRMED with glyph sizes read out of the PDF content streams via PyMuPDF — not print-media emulation). `@media (max-width: 720px)` (~line 1061) is unqualified, so it applies to **print**, and its `.tt-cell.heb, .tt-heb { font-size:1.35rem }` is a literal at the same specificity as the `font-size:var(--tt-heb-size,1.5rem)` base rules (~411/616) but later in source order — so it beats both `applyFontSizes()`'s documentElement var and the handout's `body.tt-handout-size-large/-xl` body override. A4 at Chrome's default margins is 716.9–718.1px, under the 720px breakpoint; Letter at default is 740px and escapes. **Verifier widened it twice:** it fires **unconditionally** (even at the untouched 1.5rem default, A4 prints 1.35rem), and it is **width-driven, not paper-driven** — `A4@none` (794px) prints correctly while `Letter@0.5in` (720px) prints wrong, so US teachers who pick ½-inch margins are hit too. Receipts: handout large/xl = 35.2/44.8px on Letter vs **21.6px on A4**; PDF page counts Letter 2/3/5 vs A4 2/2/2. Severity corrected P1→P2 by the verifier. Page view is immune (`.tt-bible-heb` is not in the mobile rule). **Fix chosen against BOTH agents' recommendation, on measurement:** they proposed `@media screen and (max-width:720px)`; measured on paper that also hands A4 the 24px desktop `.tt-page` padding, shrinking its text column to 670px — 24px narrower than today and 22px narrower than Letter's 692px. Today's narrower mobile padding accidentally compensates for A4's narrower paper (694 vs 692). Restating the var chain inside the print block keeps that and is exactly what the file's S30 end-of-stylesheet source-order convention exists to enable.
- [x] ✅ DONE S181, a4c581d | torah_trainer.html | **The print block's dark-token reset is incomplete, so printing from dark mode fails contrast in two places** (S181 Pass G, ink-fidelity surface). The `html.dark-early body, body.dark { … }` re-statement (~1121-1124) restates 9 tokens but omits **`--gold-text`** and **`--navy-deep`**. (a) `.tt-bnum` (Page-view verse numbers) reads `var(--gold-text)`, which the dark block sets to `var(--gold)` — printing at rgb(201,146,42) = **2.75:1** instead of light #7f5a13's 6.22:1. It is a **text** colour, so it prints on the DEFAULT path regardless of the background-graphics setting. (b) `body.dark .tt-trope-legend{background:var(--navy-deep)}` (0,2,0) out-specifies the print block's `.tt-trope-legend{background:#fff}` (0,1,0) regardless of source order, so with backgrounds ON the legend prints rgb(13,18,32) with **1.30:1** labels.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, af90983 (**gate-2 ASKED AND ANSWERED at the S182 batch — maintainer chose collapse-AND-explain over silent collapse**) | torah_trainer.html | **With the transliteration library unreachable, the printed sheet still reserves a labelled EMPTY column** (S181 Pass G, found by 2 surfaces + me). `applyTranslit()` returns `''` when the ESM has not loaded and torah_trainer has **no fallback** — unlike hebrew_dictionary, whose identical loader is passed a real corpus `defaultTranslit`. Measured: all 44 `.tt-cell.translit` cells empty while the column keeps **31.4%** of the grid width (57.5mm of 183.1mm) and its header; PDF text runs per band = English 455 / Hebrew 394 / translit **20** (verse labels only); costs an extra sheet in 2 of 2 A4 comparisons. Interlinear is worse — a literal "Translit" label over an empty row on **every** verse. Fix is small (`if (withTranslit && perVerse.every(({v}) => !applyTranslit(v.hebrew))) withTranslit = false;` before `colTranslit`/`colHeb`/`parallel` are computed — they are all computed after `perVerse`, and `.every()` short-circuits when the library IS available) but it makes a column the teacher switched ON disappear — **a default-behaviour change, hence gate 2**. Ask whether silent collapse is acceptable or it should carry a note. Real-world precondition: offline, CDN outage, or a school network blocking esm.sh/jsDelivr.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, 6896ba5 (**and the characterisation corrected — the wasted sheet carries the reading's TAIL, not nothing; 4/70 PDFs recovered a sheet, 0 regressions**) | torah_trainer.html | **A fully blank trailing sheet — about 1 print in 14** (S181 Pass G). Swept 20/24/28/32/36/40/44 verses × {interlinear, biblepage} × {trope off, on} × {Letter, A4} = **56 real PDFs**, counting text runs per page from the content streams: **4 emitted a final page with ZERO text runs** (interlinear+trope 44v Letter; biblepage 28v Letter; biblepage 32v A4; biblepage+trope 40v Letter). Cause is ~44px of trailing padding. Agent-verified fix: `.tt-page{padding-bottom:0} #ttReading{padding-bottom:0} .tt-bible-blk:last-child{margin-bottom:0} .tt-interlinear .tt-verse:last-child{padding-bottom:0}` in the print block removes all four with no regression.
- [x] ✅ DONE S182, 864c475 (**severity/direction corrected: 0–1px CLEARANCE, not a 2.5px intrusion — two independent methods; and the scope widened to side-by-side, which carries the same 1.7 leading**) | torah_trainer.html | **The printed trope clause underline cuts 2.5px into the te'amim of the line below** (S181 Pass G, interlinear only). The print rule (~1136-1140) draws `text-underline-offset:0.68em; text-decoration-thickness:4px`, but `.tt-heb` has `line-height:1.7` (40.8px) over a 33px inline box at 24px type, so the band lands outside its own line. A4 marginally worse (−3.0px vs −2.5px). Fix: `body.trope-on .tt-heb { line-height:1.95; }` inside the print block (the leading Page view already has), or drop the print offset.
- [ ] **P3 / size M (re-logged S182 — the drafted P2 one-liner is REFUTED by measurement; was P2)** | torah_trainer.html | **The לגניזה marker can still be stranded alone on its own sheet, and `break-before:avoid-page` does not stop it.** S182 shipped the drafted fix (`break-before:avoid-page; page-break-before:avoid` on the existing `body.tt-handout-geniza #ttGenizaMark` rule), measured it, and **reverted it**: with the rule shipped vs forcibly disabled the orphan count was **1/13 in BOTH arms**. - [ ] P3 | torah_trainer.html | **The reference header and the trope legend print on sheet 1 only**, so sheets 2..N of a 5–8 sheet parsha are unidentified and, with trope on, un-decodable. CSS grid cannot repeat a header band (only `table-header-group` can), so this needs a print-only running identifier. **Recommendation to weigh: repeat both compactly** — 4.7% of paper is cheaper than the blank sheet already being wasted.
- [ ] P3 | torah_trainer.html | **The nikkud print underline's intended 2px never applies.** `text-decoration: underline solid var(--nik-c) !important` is a *shorthand*, which resets `text-decoration-thickness` to `auto`; the following non-`!important` `text-decoration-thickness:2px` then loses. Proved in isolation (shorthand+!important → auto; without !important → 2px; all-longhand → 2px). Consequence: the nikkud line grows with type size until it equals the 4px trope clause line, collapsing the two-tier hierarchy at handout 'xl'. Fix: use the longhand quartet, matching the trope block's own idiom.
- [ ] P3 | torah_trainer.html | **The printed trope legend is a 35%-tint key for 100%-solid marks**, and on a greyscale printer or photocopy all six families collapse into a 16-unit grey band. Swatches read `--trope-<fam>-bg` (colour + `59` alpha) while the reading prints `--trope-<fam>-line` at full strength. Real-PDF pixel samples: swatches (240,191,189)/(187,211,234)/… vs marks (213,69,64)/(59,129,195)/…. Fix: point the six print swatches at the `-line` var they already resolve. Photocopying a class handout is the normal case, not the edge case.

Tightened to assert count stability + all-Hebrew, which then caught it. **Print boundary tested, not assumed:** a generated worksheet in blend and Name-the-Letter modes with the answer key on contains none of these names (Name-the-Letter prints `combined`, the sound). Verified EN/HE × light/dark × 1280/800 + live switch: 31/31 Hebrew, selection preserved, 0 raw keys, 0 pageerrors.

- [x] ✅ DONE S156, 9e95399 | hebrew_blend_generator.html | **Changing the transliteration style did nothing to a Real Words worksheet** (S155 Pass F dead-guard sweep; S155's naive fix deleted the answer key and was reverted). **FIXED S156** — it was **two** defects: (a) *wrong renderer* — the dead `renderCustomWordlist`/`renderRealWords` guards fell through to a `blends.length` check that is 0 in Real Words mode, so nothing re-rendered; (b) *stale cache* — `rwRenderCache` stores shallow **copies** (`words.map(w => ({...w}))`) that detach from `REAL_WORDS`, so even a correct re-render replayed the old `translitDisplay`. Fix needs a per-shape refresh (or storing word references instead of derived strings) with care around the grid snapshots. | found: 2026-07-24, S156
 `rerenderAfterTranslitChange()` tries `renderCustomWordlist` then `renderRealWords` behind `typeof … === 'function'` guards; **both functions were folded into `renderWorksheet` long ago and no longer exist**, so both guards are permanently false. The fall-through is gated on `blends.length`, which is **0 in Real Words mode** (its words never populate `blends`). Net effect: a teacher switches the style dropdown to SBL/Brill/etc. and the on-screen *and printed* Real Words sheet keeps the previous style, with no error. **Empirically confirmed** (DOM-sentinel test): Real Words → sentinel survives = no re-render; standard blends (`blends.length` 40) → re-renders correctly. **ATTEMPTED + REVERTED S155:** replacing the guards with `if (#worksheet .sheet && renderWorksheet) renderWorksheet(false)` does fix the re-render — but **`renderWorksheet(false)` called on a Real Words sheet drops the `.sheet.answer-key-sheet` page** (2 sheets → 1, verified by calling `renderWorksheet` **directly**, i.e. independent of the edit). Real Words places transliteration on a separate **answer-key page** by default (`rwTranslitPage`/`rwTrPlace = 'key'`), and a bare re-render doesn't reproduce it — so the naive fix trades a no-op for silently deleting the teacher's answer key, which is strictly worse. **The real fix must route through whatever the Generate path uses to build the answer-key page** (not bare `renderWorksheet`), or `renderWorksheet` must learn to rebuild it — that's an answer-key-pipeline change, beyond a one-concern minimal diff. | found: 2026-07-24, S155 Pass F

- [~] Item (4) ✅ DONE 2026-07-29 (out-of-band, user-directed), b158af4; item (3) remains informational-only | torah_trainer.html | **Pass G print residuals** (S53). **(4) FIXED — side-by-side prints as parallel columns again.** S53 had collapsed it to one column because the two independent block flows broke at different points and produced **wrong verse pairings** (~15 verses out of sync) — truthful, but it cost the parallel text this layout exists for. Restored without the risk: in `@media print` the column wrappers become **`display:contents`** so every cell is a direct grid item, and the builder assigns each cell an **explicit `grid-row` shared across columns**, pinning a verse's Hebrew/translit/translation to the same row so they cannot drift wherever the page breaks. **The trap that made this non-trivial:** the Hebrew column also emits **aliyah dividers**, so it has more children than its siblings and any naive row-index alignment skews — the row map allocates a row per divider (spanning all columns). With no second text there is nothing to align, so that case keeps the single-column collapse rather than reserving an empty half-page. Screen is untouched (`grid-row`/`grid-column` are inert on non-grid-items; measured screen `grid-template-columns` identical before/after). **Method worth reusing: a standalone feasibility probe ran FIRST** — a throwaway page proving Chrome fragments an explicit-placement grid correctly (60 pairs, 4 pages, none split), which de-risked the whole change before a line of production code was written. Then a **negative control** (pre-fix 70/70 rows misaligned + 1 print column → post-fix 0/70 + 3 columns) and a 10-run × light/dark matrix covering 3-col, 2-col, no-parallel, interlinear and bible page; 12-page PDF generated and the rendered page inspected. **(3) still open, and still has no fix:** nikkud letter-mode colours print but collapse to similar greys on B/W — there is no secondary channel available for vowels the way the trope layer got its print underline.

- [x] 🚫 WON'T-FIX (maintainer decision, 2026-07-22 — accept as-is) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Non-dagesh per-letter shapes share the ccmp adjacency fragility** (S115, found during the shin-dagesh fix). **DECISION: documented known limitation — no reported symptom, and the full cure is a combinatorial pl-on-form glyph blowup; not worth the glyph-count cost without a real report. (The mechanical per-mark-filtered-lookup slice was explicitly NOT taken.)** The v5.5 `ccmp_dagesh` mark-filtered lookup fixed the dagesh class, but every `builtPerLetter` rule for a NON-dagesh mark (a per-letter *vowel* shape) still emits as a plain unfiltered `sub base mark by pl_…` — unmatchable when a canonically-earlier mark intervenes, AND unreachable when the base ligates first (a per-letter qamats on bet can never fire once בּ→FB31 — would need combinatorial pl-on-form glyphs). Same disease, wider cure: per-mark filtered lookups are mechanical, but the pl-on-form combinatorics are a design call (glyph-count blowup vs. accepting the global shape on dotted letters). No reported symptom. | found: 2026-07-20, S115
- [x] 🚫 ACCEPTED S141 (user-directed backlog close — measured-fine/parked, revisit only on a real report) · P4 (pre-existing limitation, documented) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Kern pairs never cover precomposed targets** (S115 observation). Kern sides resolve stored letter cps via `gname` (`kernSide`), so a pair against ש/ב never applies to the ligated uniFB2A/uniFB31 — true before v5.5 (FB2A) and inherited unchanged by FB2C/FB2D. A fix would mirror base-letter kern classes onto their form glyphs at FEA-emit time. Cosmetic-spacing scope. | found: 2026-07-20, S115

- [ ] P4 (informational — logged, not changed) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **The template PDF is hardcoded `format:'letter'`.** `downloadTemplate` builds `new jsPDF({unit:'pt', format:'letter'})` and full-bleeds a Letter-shaped canvas onto it, so a teacher printing on **A4** gets the sheet scaled to fit (Letter 0.773 vs A4 0.707 aspect) rather than an A4-native grid. Boxes come out slightly smaller with margins — not broken, and arguably right for the US-majority audience, but it is a real difference for Israeli and European users. Deciding to add an A4 option is a product call, not a fix. | found: 2026-07-29, S169 Pass G
- [ ] P4 | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **The template PDF path could not be verified offline.** jsPDF and html2canvas are both CDN-blocked in the harness, so S169's Pass G produced artifacts through the **PNG-equivalent** route only (building the same page DOM `buildTemplatePageHTML` emits at `TPL_PAGE` size and letting the browser rasterize it). That covers layout, ink, clipping and page count — everything except jsPDF's own page assembly. A future G on this tool should either allow `cdnjs.cloudflare.com` for one run or stub jsPDF to capture the `addImage` calls. | found: 2026-07-29, S169 Pass G

_(Draw-mode intake, 2026-08-06 — deliberately out-of-scope items from the Draw-mode build (maintainer's own future-candidates list). All Font Maker; most need the Draw foundation that just shipped.)_

- [x] ✅ SHIPPED (user-directed v5.8 brush-suite extension, 2026-08-07) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Stylus-tilt control for the Chisel nib.** Fully shipped on top of the v5.8 chisel: an opt-in "Stylus tilt sets the nib angle" checkbox maps the pen's lean azimuth (`atan2(−tiltY, tiltX)` → 0–179°) onto the stroke's `na` at pen-down — frozen per stroke, a deliberate choice over the per-point channel (constant nib angle is the calligraphic contract and keeps strokes erase-split-safe); the slider stays the default for non-tilting pointers. Same build also shipped the visual brush picker (Round/Chisel/Brush/Dotted/Hollow tiles previewing the real stroke engine), speed-sensitive width for mouse/finger, and a tagin/dot/serif Stamp tool. Known tension for the Dotted style + Stamp tool: the user-triggered `doRemoveSpecks` can eat small dots/tagin next to a letter body — undoable; a future pass could skip draw-sourced glyphs. | found: 2026-08-06, Draw-mode build
- [x] ✅ SHIPPED (user-directed v5.8 draw-tools build, 2026-08-07) | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **Pixel-level eraser** (erase parts of a stroke, not the whole stroke). Shipped as the Erase tool's default **Partial** mode: the gesture accumulates an erase path with live SVG-mask feedback (array untouched mid-gesture, data-di invariant preserved), then `drawEraseSplit` resamples at ERASE_STEP pitch and splits each stroke into survivor fragments at Rt = eraser R + w/2, one undo entry, DRAW_STROKE_MAX budget so reloads never drop ink. Whole-stroke erase kept as the second sub-mode with its own `drawEraser` size pref. | found: 2026-08-06, Draw-mode build

Add a deep link that opens the generator with the just-saved font already selected. **Precedent is strong and shipped twice:** the S121 dictionary→generator Word-List link and the 2026-07-23 trope→torah `?parsha=&v=` link, both cross-tool handshake micro-features. **Likely cheap:** the generator already reads the shared `hebrewBlender_hebFont` key (registered in AllTools) and `refreshMyFonts()` resolves IndexedDB fonts at init, so this may be a **font-maker-only** change — set the key, then link. - [x] ✅ SHIPPED S162, 10bcbfa (gate-1 approved — maintainer chose **fronts + duplex backs** over the front-only split the seed suggested) | flash_cards.html | **Print a deck as a cut-out card sheet** (S159 Pass G seed). Setup-screen "Print card sheet" button lays the generated deck out **3×3 per page with dashed cut lines**; the `sheetDuplex` toggle appends an answer page after each card page. **The duplex mapping is the load-bearing part:** flipping on the long edge reverses left-right, so each ROW is reversed on the answer page, and a short final row is **padded to full width BEFORE reversing** — without the pad, a 2-card row reverses into the wrong columns and every answer on it lands behind the wrong card. One builder covers all modes because every card is `{heb, translit, …}`: Hebrew blends, numbers, colours (cell painted from `colorCss` + `print-color-adjust`; only a literal hex/rgb may reach the style attribute) and emoji all verified. **89 lines** — inside the ~150 ceiling even with duplex, so no split was needed. `sheetDuplex` wired into `getSettings()`/`applySettings()` with `??`, riding the already-registered `hebrewFlashCards_settings` blob (no new AllTools keys).

Presence is also the **only side-effect-free probe available** — testing visibility would mean running every step's `reveal()`, which in the dictionary opens the sidebar and expands panels the tour hasn't reached (the exact artifact this pass's own first probe hit). The step being shown always counts itself, so torah's `_tourHoldForReading` (parked on a step whose target hasn't rendered) still numbers correctly. **torah `1,2,3,6 of 6` → `1,2,3,4 of 4`; dictionary `1,2,3,4,6,7 of 7` → `1..6 of 6`; both unchanged when the content loads.** Verified 12/12 matrix cells (light/dark × 1280/800 × three states): no jump, stable total, walk ends exactly at that total, 0 pageerrors, 0 vacuous runs; the 5 untouched tours unchanged.

- [x] 2026-08-11 | f8a604f | Hebrew_Font_Maker.html | **QA Check stops warning about the app's own built-in comma** — Pass H's headline find, and a **P3 that fires in 100% of projects ever created**. The outline scan reported "⚠ 2 glyphs with self-intersecting or overlapping outlines: **Comma, Semicolon**" on a project where **nothing had been drawn at all** — both are the app's own built-in punctuation (`srcKind:'single'`, `traced:false`, `hasDraw:false`). **The overlap is real but deliberate and harmless:** the comma's tail is tucked into its round head so the join looks seamless, and its two contours are **both outer** (`winding:['cw','cw']`; Semicolon `['cw','cw','cw']`), which the **nonzero fill rule unions** — no inversion is possible. The remedy the message offered ("edit the nodes or re-trace to fix") was not even available for a glyph nobody drew. The overlap loop now skips a pair **only when both contours are outer**; **outer/hole and hole/hole overlaps — the cases that actually cancel a region — still fire** (hole/hole was added deliberately: two overlapping counters double-subtract and invert, so "windings differ" alone would have been a narrower rule than correctness allows). A permanent unclearable warning is how a teacher learns to **ignore the panel that also carries the real findings about her own letters**. | verified headless light+dark × 1280/800, pageerror==0, 44 checks: untouched project raises **0** outline warnings and the QA panel keeps its placement verdict; **positive controls** confirm outer+hole and hole+hole still flag, self-intersection still detected (its call signature changed), normal counters + disjoint contours still clear, and a contour with **no** `winding` field still defaults to outer.

### Pattern health (per recurring pattern: last swept, hits that sweep, consecutive clean sweeps; detail in the sweep log below)
- **animation-outside-its-reduced-motion-block** (**NEW, registered S198**): a CSS `transition`/`animation` declaration whose selector is **not covered by its own page's `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block** — so a reader who asked the OS for less motion still gets that one. CLAUDE.md makes the coverage binding ("if you add an animation to a page, that page needs the reduced-motion block, and your animation must honor it"), which is exactly why a *partial* block is the dangerous shape: the block's presence reads as compliance. **Detection (cheap, mechanical, no rendering needed):** for each file, collect every selector carrying a `transition:`/`animation:` declaration outside the reduced-motion block, then subtract the selectors named inside it; anything left is a candidate. **Confirm at runtime with a control arm** — read `transitionDuration` under `reducedMotion:'reduce'` **and** `'no-preference'`; a probe that reports `0s` in both states is measuring nothing. **The sibling heuristic is what found S198's hit:** elements that *travel together* (a drawer and its backdrop, a modal and its scrim) must be neutralized together, and a block naming one but not the other is the tell. Swept 2026-08-12 (S198): **1 hit / 3 carriers of the settings drawer** — `torah_trainer` (fixed 915526d); `classroom_dashboard` and `trope_tutor` already correct. **Not yet swept suite-wide** — S198 censused only the `.settings-backdrop` shape; the full 11-page selector-subtraction sweep is the next A run's job. ACTIVE, clean streak 0.
- **content-dependent-tour-step-miscounts-the-tour** (**NEW, registered S197**): a guided-tour step whose `target()` only exists once remote/corpus content has rendered. The engines all skip an unresolvable step **silently by design** ("skip gracefully when hidden"), but the step counter is built from the **raw array index**, so on any failed/absent load the number **jumps and over-promises** — the teacher watches steps vanish. **Detection (cheap, ~2 min/tool): walk the real tour on a fresh profile and compare steps SHOWN against `TOUR_STEPS.length`; then re-walk with that tool's own corpus route-aborted.** The second arm is what makes it discriminating — the first arm alone only catches tools whose content is EXTERNAL (torah/Sefaria), and silently passes every tool served from local `data/`. **Fix shape: count by PRESENCE of each target, never visibility** — absent = content never arrived; hidden = a collapsed panel that opens fine. Presence is also the only side-effect-free probe: testing visibility means running every step's `reveal()`, which in the dictionary opens the sidebar and expands panels the tour hasn't reached. FIRST sweep 2026-08-12 (S197 Pass I), all 7 tour-bearing tools: **2 carriers found, both fixed → 0 remaining** (`torah_trainer.html`, `hebrew_dictionary.html`). **trope_tutor is the documented NEGATIVE — it has a content-dependent step too (`#tuCards .tu-card`) yet holds 6/6 with data blocked**, which is what proves the detector isn't just reporting "blocking data breaks tours". The other 4 tools target static chrome only. **Clean streak: 0 (first sweep, hits found).** ACTIVE.
- **per-sample-repaint-of-an-O(n)-live-preview** (**NEW, registered S196**): a continuous gesture — `pointermove`, a slider drag — that rebuilds an **O(n) preview from its whole accumulated buffer once per input SAMPLE** rather than once per animation frame, so the gesture is O(n²) in its own length. The screen cannot show more than one repaint per frame, so every extra rebuild is pure waste, and the cost is invisible at the short strokes/small drags a developer tests with. **Detection:** find the expensive builder call inside a `pointermove`/`oninput` handler where the builder walks a whole array; then, under **PACED** input (yield to rAF between batches — a synchronous dispatch loop starves rAF and fakes a 100× win), count builder invocations against frames. A per-sample handler shows `invocations == accepted samples`; a correct one shows `invocations ≈ frames`. Confirm with a first-10%-vs-last-10% mean ratio per sample — a flat ratio is clean, a rising one is the class. **Fix idiom (already in-tree twice):** `boldGenSet` and `nodeStatusCoords` — coalesce with an rAF token on the gesture object, re-read state at frame time (never snapshot), and guard the callback with `gesture !== g` so an ended gesture paints nothing. **Found + fixed 2026-08-12 (S196, Pass D): 1 hit** — Font Maker's four styled draw pens (2e09c29); its round pen is the same shape but measured under budget and deliberately left (logged as a Candidate with the two inputs that would make it actionable). **⚑ Notable: S85's Pass D fixed this exact shape in the same tool** (Generate Bold's per-frame warning scan), and Draw mode reintroduced it 111 sessions later — so this class recurs *within* a tool as new gesture surfaces are built. Clean streak: 0 (first sweep). Correctness-adjacent (degrades the primary drawing interaction), not consequence-critical.
- **false-positive-validator-on-the-app's-own-content** (**NEW, registered S194**): a QA/lint/warning rule whose **detector is broader than the failure it warns about**, so it fires on artwork or data the app itself ships — the user cannot act on it, cannot clear it, and it is present in **every** project, which is precisely how a panel of real warnings gets trained into background noise. Founding instance: the Font Maker outline scan flagging its own built-in **Comma** and **Semicolon** (`glyphOutlineScan` tested "do two contours' edges cross" while the message promised "can fill **inverted**" — inversion needs *opposite* winding, and both glyphs are all-outer, so nonzero simply unions them). **Detection:** for each validator, run it on a **pristine/empty project** — anything it reports there is by definition not the user's doing. Cheap (one page-evaluate) and it generalizes past contrast/i18n to every warning surface: FM QA, the export guard, the dictionary/flash-cards load states. **Second question once a hit is found:** is the right fix to *exempt the built-in* or to *narrow the detector*? Narrowing is usually correct and fixes the class — but check the narrowing does not silently drop a real case (here, "windings differ" would have missed hole/hole double-subtraction, so the rule became "skip only when BOTH are outer"). swept 2026-08-11 **S194**, hits: 1, clean streak: 0 — **ACTIVE**
- **JSON-LD ↔ visible-content parity** (**UN-RETIRED 2026-08-08, S190** — retired at S64, re-check ownership moved A2→Pass L at S142, and L's 5th run found it recurred): an `application/ld+json` FAQ/HowTo/ItemList claim that no longer describes the shipped tool. **Detection, as narrowed by S190:** a hit is (a) a **closed enumeration** that asserts the complete set of something the UI has since grown ("one per image, a full sheet, or an existing font file" vs the wizard's four sources), or (b) a description of **how a feature works** that no longer describes it (the dashboard's "assign a preset and an end time to each period" vs the weekly grid). *Merely incomplete prose is still NOT a hit* — that part of S142's convention stands. **Always check the VISIBLE twin**: every claim on these pages has an on-page FAQ counterpart, and S190 found all three twins carrying the identical stale wording in EN and HE, so a JSON-LD-only fix would create a new mismatch. Swept 2026-08-08 (S190), **hits: 3** (font-maker, dashboard, dictionary — all fixed 3237da8), clean streak: **0** — ACTIVE. Re-checked in Pass L. Named unswept surface for the next L: `index.html`'s `ItemList` of 8 tools, the 7 `WebApplication` blocks' `featureList` where present, and the generator/flash-cards/torah/trope FAQ+HowTo blocks (S174 hand-verified flash-cards; the rest were checked under the OLD non-enumeration convention and deserve a pass under the narrowed one).
- **wired-then-clobbered label** (NEW, registered S192): an element that carries a **correct `data-i18n` binding** and is then **overwritten by a JS setter with a hardcoded English literal** — so `applyStaticI18n` translates it and the setter immediately reverts it. Distinct from `pre-ready-i18n / never-re-rendered` (a builder that runs before `I18n.ready` and is never re-run): here the key IS referenced and the wiring IS correct; a later writer clobbers it. **Why both gates are blind to it:** the literals characteristically sit in a **ternary** (`c ? 'Edit kern class' : 'New kern class'`) or an **array** rather than a plain assignment, which is exactly Check A's documented blind spot; and Check B only measures CSV translation coverage, which is complete. **Detection (repeatable, cheap):** build a map of `id → data-i18n key` from the file's static markup, then scan every `.textContent`/`.innerHTML`/`.value`/`.placeholder` assignment whose RHS contains an English string literal and no `I18n.t(`, resolving the target element by the nearest preceding `getElementById` within ~12 lines. **Triage rule learned on the first run:** that ID-proximity heuristic mismatches — 2 of 4 raw candidates were the generator's printed worksheet subtitles attributed to an unrelated `worksheet.layout.title_aria` binding, and printed output is never-translate anyway, so **always confirm the resolved element really is the bound one before logging**. **Fix shape:** retarget the binding as well as the text (`setAttribute('data-i18n', k)` + `textContent = I18n.t(k)`) — the existing suite idiom at `flash_cards.html` `timerShowLabel`. That is what makes a language switch land, and it avoids the trap of "just add the function to `applyI18n`", which is **illegal** when the function mutates state (`applyI18n` must stay read-only). Guard the text write against the pre-ready case (`t()` returns the key before locales load) so a raw dot-key can never be painted. Swept 2026-08-11 (S192, first sweep): **4 raw / 2 real / 2 fixed / 0 left**, across all 13 root HTML files. Clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE.
- **theme-flipping-token-on-a-fixed-colour-plate** (NEW, registered S191): a control or text node styled with a colour token that **inverts with the theme** — `var(--text)`, `var(--white)`, `var(--muted)` — placed inside a container painted a **fixed** colour that does not invert (a `--navy`/`--navy-deep` plate, a gold button, a print sheet). One theme composites fine and the other collapses toward 1:1, so a single-theme check clears it and the failure lands in whichever theme the author was not looking at. **This is the documented `--white` pitfall in CLAUDE.md read in the other direction** — that note warns `var(--white)` text disappears on navy in DARK; the S191 hit was `var(--text)` disappearing on navy in **LIGHT** (the bulk dock's `.btn-secondary`, measured 1:1, label rendered as an empty box). **Detection is not greppable** — the token and the plate are in different rules — so it needs the alpha-compositing contrast probe run in **both** themes, with transparent backgrounds walked up to an opaque ancestor. The house fix is the existing navy-plate idiom: literal `#fff` on `rgba(255,255,255,0.12)`. **Consequence-critical (a control whose label is invisible), so it does not retire on a clean streak.** — ACTIVE **2nd sweep 2026-08-11 (S193 Pass A): 1 hit, fixed (cd52e15).** Clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE. **Definition widened: the class has a MIRROR IMAGE** — S191's was an inverting token on a fixed plate; trope_tutor's is a **fixed** colour (the UA default `#0000EE` on an anchor no rule reached) on a plate that **inverts**. Same failure and same 1–2:1 floor, opposite cause; the compositing detector finds both because it measures the result, not the tokens. Detail + the new one-evaluate sub-detector in the sweep log below. **⚑ S194: 1 more hit on a NEW carrier via the S193 UA-default-anchor detector** — the Font Maker's 3 `.hint` anchors (`#0000EE`, **1.81:1** on the dark Advanced panel), fixed 01ebdb5 with the same `--gold-text` one-liner. That makes **3 carriers in 4 sessions** (torah_trainer dock, trope_tutor About, FM hints) and confirms the detector's value: it is one page-evaluate, needs no compositing baseline, and the misses are always anchors the page simply never styled because a `footer a`/`.panel a` rule did not reach them. **Next carriers to sweep: the 5 dormant anchors the S193 run found un-rendered** — enable each one's disclosure state first, which is exactly what turned the FM hint links from "never measured" into a confirmed 1.81:1.
- **two-state-ready-flag-for-a-three-state-load** (NEW, registered S189): a lazily-fetched corpus or module whose UI decides what to render from a single truthiness check — `REAL_WORDS.length`, `EMOJI_DATA`, `_wordsReady`. **That flag has two states; the fetch has three** (never attempted / in flight / failed), so the "not ready" arm is also what a FAILED load renders, permanently: either a confident false zero ("0 matching words" — worse than silence, because it is a claim about the user's own selection) or a "Loading…" that never ends, refreshed by every later re-render and language switch. **Detection:** for each lazy fetch, name its ready flag, grep every render that branches on it, and ask what the failure path leaves on screen; then stall the resource AND 502 it headless, reading the end state **after ≥4 s** — a short sample cannot tell "stuck" from "not yet". **Run both languages:** the dictionary instance PASSED in English and FAILED in Hebrew, because the later locale fetch changes whether a re-render lands after the catch. Also check whether the FIRST write happens *before* the fetch exists — the generator's guard was correct and still never fired, because every trigger path painted the caption before calling the loader (fix at the loader, not the call site). Swept 2026-08-08 (S189, Pass F) over the primary slow operation of all 7 tools, **hits: 3 of 7** (generator, flash cards, dictionary — the last with a twin instance two lines away in the same function), **all fixed same-session**; clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE. **Unswept sibling surfaces named for the next A:** the four transliteration-ESM status lines (`#translitLoadStatus` in generator/flash cards/dictionary, `#optTranslitStatus` in torah), the dashboard's `fetchShabbat`, and torah_trainer's `manifestData`/`parshiyotData` loads. **Re-swept 2026-08-11 (S193 Pass A): CLEAN — all 3 S189 hits fixed, three-state idiom now in place. Clean streak: 1 — ACTIVE.**
- **invalid-SVG-geometry-from-an-unclamped-difference** (NEW, registered S187): an SVG `width`/`height` computed as the difference of two mapped coordinates (`fx(b) - fx(a)`, `x1 - x0`) where one side comes from a **derived** value that can legitimately go negative — so the attribute goes negative, and per spec the browser **does not render the element at all** and logs an error. The overlay silently disappears in exactly the unusual case it was there to explain, and the console error repeats on every render. **Sibling-asymmetry is the tell:** one of a paired accessor clamps (`letterRSB` → `Math.max(0, …)`) and the other forgot to (`letterLSB`). **Detection:** wrap the `innerHTML` setter and regex added markup for `<rect[^>]*(?:width|height)="-` — the raw console count UNDERSTATES the frequency because Chromium collapses repeated identical messages (measured S187: 4 real assignments, 1 console line). Then check every `width="${…}"` built as a difference. **Swept 2026-08-08 (S187) over Hebrew_Font_Maker's 21 rect emitters: 1 hit (`marginGuidesSVG`'s left zone, fixed 74e0530, + the identical `wideFormGuidesSVG` shape guarded), rest clean** — `node-marquee` uses `Math.abs`, `markSelectRect` clamps to MIN 22, the capture-box rect was measured positive, and the handle/point rects are fixed-size. **Not yet swept elsewhere** — the obvious carriers are the generator's worksheet cut-line/QR geometry and torah_trainer's trope-underline print rules. clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE. **Re-swept 2026-08-11 (S193 Pass A): CLEAN — and both named carriers emit ZERO `<rect>`, so the shape is Font-Maker-only in practice. Clean streak: 1 — ACTIVE.**
- **RTL-inheritance-on-a-Latin-script-container** (NEW, registered S185): a container whose CONTENT is Latin script — an English translation, a transliteration, a Latin-labelled readout — that declares no `direction`, so under the Hebrew UI (`<html dir="rtl">`) it inherits an RTL base direction and every sentence-final period, comma or bracket jumps to the wrong end. Silent in the English UI, silent to `check-i18n` (nothing is untranslated), and it reaches PAPER as well as screen. **Detection:** render each layout in the HE UI and compare `getComputedStyle(el).direction` against the script of the text the element actually carries; assert under `emulateMedia({media:'print'})` too. **⚠ DETECTION AMENDED S187 — the computed-`direction` test alone OVER-REPORTS badly.** Swept over trope_tutor (the tool the S186 pointer named as the unswept carrier): **29 Latin-carrying leaves all compute `direction:rtl`, and per-character `Range` x-position comparison in EN vs HE found 0 of 7 punctuated strings visually reordered** — a single uninterrupted LTR run keeps logical order under an RTL base regardless (bracket pairs included, BD16). So the **mandatory second stage is a visual-order comparison**; a hit requires punctuation that can actually move, which in practice means **sentence-final** punctuation at the END of the run (what the real f5a0d72 hit had), not punctuation enclosed inside it. Counting computed-direction alone would have manufactured 29 false hits here. **Swept 2026-08-06 (S185) over torah_trainer's three reading layouts: 1 hit (side-by-side's two Latin columns, fixed f5a0d72), 2 clean** — interlinear pins `.tt-translit`/`.tt-translation` itself and Page view carries `dir="ltr"` on its bible blocks. **Not yet swept anywhere else** — the obvious carriers are the generator's worksheet/answer-key transliteration cells, flash-cards' card faces, and the dictionary's English/transliteration rows, all of which render Latin content inside a page that flips to `dir="rtl"`. **Pin on the ROW/cell, not the column**: a column-level pin also flips translated `<h3>` chrome, which is a regression (measured S185 — the drafted one-liner in the S181 candidate would have done exactly that). **trope_tutor swept CLEAN 2026-08-08 (S187), screen AND print.** clean streak: 1 — ACTIVE.
- **silent-external-media-failure** (an `<audio>`/`<video>`/media element pointed at a third-party host with play/pause/ended handlers wired but **no `error` handler**, so a blocked or dead origin produces silence while the controls still show a live playing state. Detection: answer the media URL with an error response — `route.abort()` leaves the element STALLED rather than errored, so it never fires — and drive the real play path, because a `preload="none"` element fetches nothing until `play()`. Any control still reading "stop"/"pause" afterwards is a hit.) — **registered + first swept 2026-08-02 (S183)** on `torah_trainer.html` only: **1 hit** (the PocketTorah MP3, fixed a6bf328), clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE. **Unswept elsewhere:** the trope tutor's `#tuAudio` clip engine plays the same PocketTorah MP3s and has its own `_badFiles`/`onError` path that should be checked against this definition, and the dashboard's YouTube iframe is a third-party media embed with no error surface at all. **Swept 2026-08-08 (S187) over trope_tutor's `#tuAudio` clip engine — the surface the S186 pointer named as the unswept carrier: CLEAN, and MEASURED rather than read.** Per S183's method note the MP3 was fulfilled with a **502** (an `abort` leaves the element stalled, not errored): `MediaError.code 4`, `_badFiles.size 2`, a visible localized `role="status"` line "Audio unavailable right now — check your connection.", 0 pageerror. `playClip` carries a per-load `error` listener **and** a 20 s timeout for the black-holed-network case the pattern names, plus a token guard and seek verification — the reference implementation for this class.
- **print-trailing-dead-space** (padding/margin BELOW the last line of a print flow — page-container bottom padding, a scroll wrapper's, the last block's own margin — which paginates exactly like content, so a document ending near a page boundary pushes empty box onto a sheet of its own. Detection: under `emulateMedia({media:'print'})` at the true printable width, compare the page container's `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` against the deepest bottom edge of any text-bearing descendant; any positive delta is dead space that can cost a sheet. Confirm on real PDFs, never on emulation alone.) — **registered + first swept 2026-08-02 (S182)** on `torah_trainer.html` only: **1 hit** (22–44px depending on layout/paper, fixed 6896ba5), clean streak: 0 — ACTIVE. **The other 11 root pages have never been checked** — `hebrew_blend_generator.html` and `flash_cards.html` both have heavy print paths and are the obvious next targets.
- **lazily-loaded-dependency-renders-an-empty-shell** (a feature whose data comes from a lazily-loaded external module keeps rendering its full chrome — column, header, row label, legend — when the module never arrives, so the user gets a labelled void with the toggle still reading ON. - **print-media-leak / var-chain-overridden-by-a-literal** (a screen-only `@media (max-width:N)` block whose declarations also apply to PAPER — print media has a width too — or, more generally, a literal `font-size`/colour declaration that out-specifies a `var(--x)` chain the UI's own controls feed. Original definition: (a store the tool's `IVRIT_CFG.apply()` is fully able to restore, but `gather()` never writes — so the tool's own `.ivrit` backup silently omits it, while the index.html AllTools bundle carries it correctly): **registered + swept 2026-07-25 (S159, Pass G), hits: 2 of the 3 `.ivrit` tools.** (1) **flash_cards — student profiles, P1 data-loss (FIXED d7a6f23):** `apply()` reads `data.flashCardProfiles` and calls `mergeProfilesBlob`, with a comment saying it restores profiles "when the file includes them" — `gather()` never included them, so the file carried `profileFolders` **with none of the profiles inside**. Restoring on a new machine returned empty folders and lost every student's drill history. (2) **generator — `hebFont` + `livePreview`, P3 (FIXED 3a06669):** `getSettings()` carried `hebFontSize` but not the font itself, so a restored worksheet used the right size in the default face. (3) **dashboard — CLEAN** (its settings blob is exactly what `liveState` already carries). clean streak: 0 — **ACTIVE, consequence-critical** (backup completeness / data-loss-on-new-machine, the same carve-out as localStorage-vs-AllTools). **Why the existing sweep missed it:** localStorage-vs-AllTools checks keys against **index.html's three functions**, and both gaps were correctly registered *there* — the per-tool `.ivrit` payload is a second, independent registry that nothing was checking. **Detection (cheap, headless):** for each page with an `IVRIT_CFG`, call `IVRIT_CFG.gather()` live and diff `Object.keys(result)` + `Object.keys(result.liveState)` against the keys that tool owns in the AllTools table; anything owned-but-absent is a candidate. Then confirm by round-trip — seed real state, export, wipe, `IVRIT_CFG.apply(parsed.data, 'merge')`, and assert the state came back. **Read `apply()` first:** in both hits `apply()` was already correct, so the whole defect was one missing line in `gather()`. **Re-swept 2026-07-29 (S168), hits: 0** — all four `IVRIT_CFG` carriers checked; `apply()` and `gather()` are aligned, and the apparent gaps are the documented AllTools-bundle **alias** keys (`data.presets || data.flashCardPresets`). - **symbol-only accessible name** (an icon-only control whose ENTIRE accessible name is a glyph with no letter or digit — "×", "↺", "✕", "⬛", an emoji run — because for `button`/`a`/`role=button` the ACCNAME chain takes **name-from-content BEFORE `title`**, so a correct translated `title` on the very same element is never reached. A screen reader announces "multiplication sign, button" or, for an unmapped glyph, nothing): **registered + swept suite-wide 2026-07-29 (S167, Pass C), hits: 50 across 6 tools** — dashboard 27, font-maker 13, generator 4, flash-cards 5, dictionary 3, torah 2; trope_tutor, index, resources, contact, privacy and 404 **clean**. **All 50 fixed** (ddc9180 + 2c4b78f): 38 were pure wiring (mirror the existing translated `title` into `aria-label`), 12 had no label at all and took 9 new keys + 3 reuses. clean streak: 0 — **ACTIVE, consequence-critical** (a11y blocker, and it re-appears every time someone adds an icon button). **Detection:** compute the ACCNAME chain in-page over the focusable set and flag `name && !/\p{L}|\p{N}/u.test(name)` — but run it BOTH ways, because each method misses what the other catches: the runtime sweep missed the generator's two collapsed-section resets and every overlay-only button, while a static `<button ...>INNER</button>` scan missed all 4 dashboard sites built by the `mk()` helper and needed the modals driven open to be believed. **Two exemptions, deliberate:** a `div[role="tab"]`/`role="button"` whose content is real text IS named (name-from-content) — a hand-rolled shim that only allows content for `button`/`a` reports these as nameless, which is how S167 briefly "found" a nonexistent Font-Maker defect; and a glyph label that is genuinely decorative beside visible text is fine. **Fix rule:** when mirroring inside a JS builder, gate it on the label having no letters, so a worded button keeps its visible text as its name (WCAG 2.5.3 Label in Name). **Re-swept 2026-08-01 (S179, Pass A): 2 hits, logged not fixed** — the My Fonts row buttons in BOTH carriers (`renderMyFontsList` in the Font Maker, `renderMyFontsManager`'s `mk()` helper in index.html): `⬇`/`🗑` textContent with a correctly-translated `title` that name-from-content never reaches.
