The seed queue's remaining S-sized dual-audience pick is the **dashboard weekly-grid print seed**; the torah_trainer seeds from S180 are numerous and untouched. **Offer a non-font-maker, non-trope-tutor seed first** given the coverage above — the dashboard grid seed fits, and dashboard is stale (S196).
**Method notes new in S198:**
(1) **A style read is not a visibility read when the ancestor is the one that is hidden.** `getComputedStyle(btn).display` returns the button's OWN `inline-flex` even when its parent is `display:none`, so the print-media arm reported both buttons visible on a sheet they were provably absent from. Rects go to zero and are the honest instrument. **The PDF settled it in one command** — the extracted text contained neither button — which is the S195 rule holding again: for print, a style probe is a hypothesis and the artifact is the evidence.
(2) **Reuse the real renderer instead of duplicating it, and pay for it with ONE guard.** The print chart could have re-implemented `renderLearn`'s ~95-line card builder; walking every family through the live renderer instead kept the diff at 71 lines and makes screen/paper drift impossible by construction. The whole cost was a single `_chartPrintBuild` flag suppressing one side effect. **Before duplicating a builder, ask what would have to be suppressed to reuse it** — usually it is one line, and the answer is a better fix.
(3) **A side effect that writes to a STUDENT'S record is the thing to look for before reusing a renderer.** `renderLearn` marks a family 'visited' in mastery progress; a naive print-all would have silently marked all six studied. That arm — snapshot `progress.families` before/after — is the one in the harness that could genuinely fail, and it is the one worth writing first.
(4) **The maintainer's clarifying question can be worth more than the answer to the question you asked.** Gate 2 offered 'add the header Tour button, with or without the first-visit pulse'; the reply was a question — *'what about the wizard — doesn't that override that?'* Grounding it split the answer cleanly: the wizard does **not** cover the gap (unchecked by default, and never shown to a returning teacher whose autosave restores) but **does** override the pulse — and chasing it surfaced a trap neither of us had raised, that this page writes its flag on tour **completion** rather than **start**, so a pulse gated on it would have returned every visit until a teacher finished all seven steps. **Answer the question with file evidence before re-offering the choice.**
(5) **A clean sweep is only trustworthy if its weak arms were caught and re-run.** Two of this pass's eight arms were vacuous on the first pass (scroll measured step 1 only at a height where the target was already visible; pulse was measured without a flag-set control). Both would have been reported as 'clean' — and the re-run versions are what produced the 14/14 spotlight receipt and the six-carrier pulse convergence. **Ask of every clean arm: could this have failed?**
(6) **`pip install pypdf` + the `sys.modules` cryptography stub still works** (S195 note 6, re-confirmed 2026-08-12, pypdf 6.15.0). Per-page text extraction is the only honest way to check a printed artifact for blank pages and stray chrome; **print per-page character counts**, not just totals, or a stranded page hides inside a healthy-looking total.
(7) **`document.querySelectorAll('body *')` includes `<script>`, and a script element's child text node is the entire inline JS source.** The close-out's raw-i18n-key sweep reported **12 of 12 cells failing** on keys like `shared.darkmode.label_light` and `torah.confirm.reset_all` — every one of them an `I18n.t('…')` **call site** in the page's own source, not a string on screen. `:not(script):not(style)` cleared all 12. Any on-screen-text detector on these single-file pages needs that exclusion, or it re-reports the whole application as page content — and note the failure mode is **loud and plausible**, since real raw keys look exactly like this.
(8) **Playwright's `page.pdf()` does NOT fire `beforeprint`.** A feature wired to that event must be triggered explicitly before the PDF is taken, and the event path verified separately by dispatching it — otherwise the artifact arm silently tests a different code path than the one real users hit.
**Method notes new in S197 — the pass produced two confident wrong results and both were caught by re-measuring rather than by reasoning:**
(1) **Resolving a list of steps by looping over all of them is not the same as resolving them one at a time, in order.** A static probe that called every step's `reveal()` in sequence reported the dashboard's `#movePanelsBtn` step as unresolvable — because each later `reveal()` collapses the panel an earlier one opened. The **real tour walk showed 8/8**. When a probe and the actual engine disagree, the engine's own path is the authoritative arm; a snapshot loop over stateful setup functions is not a model of a sequential UI.
(2) **A harness that blocks external origins will manufacture a "no content" finding on any tool whose content is external — and hide it on any tool whose content is local.** torah_trainer's tour drop looked like the universal fresh-teacher state until the status line (`"Failed to load Bereshit… Failed to fetch"`) proved the page *had* auto-selected a parsha and my own route-abort had starved it. The finding survived, but only as the **load-failure** state, and the correction is what turned one instance into a class: re-running with each tool's OWN corpus blocked found the dictionary carrier that the first pass had sailed straight past. **State the condition a finding depends on, then go looking for who else meets it.**
(3) **A negative control is what separates a class from an artifact.** trope_tutor has a content-dependent tour step too and holds 6/6 with its data blocked. Without that cell, "blocking data breaks tours" would have been indistinguishable from the real claim.
(4) **Read the property before believing the reading.** `project.name` came back `undefined` after the wizard and looked like the teacher's font name being dropped; the name lives at `project.font.familyName`. Two minutes in `wizardCreate()` beat a plausible bug report.
(5) **A `*/` inside a JS block comment ends the comment.** `setMarkEnabled/setAdd*/setInputMode` in a harness header silently truncated the file into a syntax error. Cheap to fix, easy to misread as a Playwright problem.
(6) **Version numbering is a repo convention, not a guess.** 5.9's successor here is **5.10**, not 6.0 — the v4 line ran to v4.31. One `grep -o '<li><strong>v[0-9.]*'` answered it.
(7) **The HTTP server dies across long tool gaps in this container.** It dropped twice mid-session (`ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, once mid-verification). Re-`curl` it before each harness run rather than trusting that it is still up — the failure mode looks like a page bug for the first few seconds.

**Method notes new in S195:**
(1) **Re-derive a number even when the ledger is the one that wrote it.** S194's pointer named "flash_cards or the generator" for this G. That came from the S169 *tally*, which counts G fixes rather than dedicated runs — so the tool with a true **zero** was invisible in it. The pointer itself said "check the per-run history before committing", and doing so changed the target. A tally and a history can disagree; prefer the history and record which you used.
(2) **When the instrument that finds a defect cannot see the fix, change instruments — and prove the new one discriminates.** The rect-based straddle model found the split cards, but `break-inside` moves the printer's break without moving any DOM geometry, so that model reports identical numbers before and after. Verification moved to per-page PDF text, and the **control run on the pre-fix PDFs** (`[(4,2),(1,3),(0,0)]` unbalanced) is what makes the post-fix `[(2,2),(2,2),(1,1)]` mean anything. A verification that cannot fail on the broken build is not a verification.
(3) **For print, a style probe is a hypothesis and the PDF is the evidence.** Three of four style-probe alarms this session were artifacts: a closed `translateX(100%)` settings drawer still has client rects (it does not print), and an off-sheet `top:-48px` skip-link still computes 1:1 white-on-white. Only elements confirmed on the rendered artifact should be logged. The converse also held — the probe's *ink* measurements were exactly right, because "colour vs white paper" is a property of the sheet, not of layout.
(4) **A first-run assertion that fails a correct fix is the norm, not the exception — budget for it.** Two of this session's four fixes had a verification arm fail before the fix did: `.tu-card`'s plate is `var(--cream)` and not `var(--white)` (guessed), and the "Translit / Translation" row legitimately wraps to 48px over 22px children **on the unmodified file too**. Both were settled the same cheap way — `git stash`, re-measure the baseline, compare. **Run the stash check from the repo root**: it failed once this session (and once in S194) purely because cwd was the scratchpad.
(5) **A tool with no print BUTTON still has a print SURFACE.** trope_tutor has zero `window.print()` calls, which is exactly why it went 195 sessions without a print audit — but it ships an `@media print` block, and Ctrl+P on a reference chart is a thing teachers do. **Census printable surface by `@media print` OR an export path, never by the presence of a print button.**
(6) **`pip install` works in this container, but the system `cryptography` has broken Rust bindings.** pypdf imports it only for encrypted PDFs; stubbing the module tree in `sys.modules` before the import gives working per-page PDF text extraction, which is the only honest way to verify pagination. Worth remembering for any future G run.

**Method notes new in S193:**
(1) **Name a carrier from a count, never from what a file appears to do.** The `invalid-SVG` entry named the generator and torah_trainer as its next carriers; both emit **zero** `<rect>` elements. In the same session the `.btn-xs` candidate said "7 of 8 files"; only 5 carry the class. Both errors are the same shape — a plausible-sounding scope written down without running the one command that would check it — and both cost a sweep's worth of misdirection.
(2) **A registered pattern can have a mirror image, and the detector usually finds it while the definition does not.** `theme-flipping` was defined as an inverting token on a fixed plate. The instance found was the reverse. The compositing detector caught it because it measures the **composite result**, not the tokens — which is the same "measure the symptom, not the cause" move S192 made for i18n, arriving independently in a second class. When a definition and its detector disagree about what counts, trust the detector and widen the definition.
(3) **When a live region is added, guard the write.** `updateCopyTextarea` is called by five display toggles and by `applyI18n`, not only by selection changes, so an unguarded `textContent =` would have announced the count for flipping an unrelated checkbox. **Measure announcements with a MutationObserver, not by reading the text** — the text is right either way; only the mutation count shows the spurious speech.
(4) **A cheap detector is worth writing down even when it finds little.** The UA-default-anchor check (`computed colour ∈ 3 known values`) is one page-evaluate, needs no compositing or baseline, and covers the entire suite in ~90 s. It found 2 real hits and 5 dormant ones. It now lives on the pattern so the next A run gets it free.
(5) **A vacuous test arm is invisible unless you print it.** The verse-count harness reported `display-toggle=n/a` because its selector matched no element — the single most important silence test had not run. It was only visible because the arm prints its own status rather than folding into a pass/fail. Print what each arm actually did, every time.
(6) **Check whether the pre-existing code already answers the question before calling new code a bug.** The out-of-band `fmtClock()`'s hardcoded `'en-US'` looked like a finding until the other five locale calls on the page — including the big clock that shipped long ago — all turned out to hardcode it too. The convention was the answer, and it is documented in CLAUDE.md.

**Method notes new in S192 — the first two are the ones with life beyond this pass:**
(1) **When a detector keeps disagreeing with itself, switch from measuring the cause to measuring the symptom.** Three static censuses across two sessions gave 721/2/424/1082/677 for the same question. The runtime version — English on screen in the Hebrew UI, reverse-matched to `en.json` — gave 35 hits with a candidate key attached to each, and its ONLY failure mode is a coincidental cross-concept string match, which a human clears in seconds (`Letter` the paper size vs the colour mode; `Default` a user's preset name; `Alef` a font family, not the glyph — that one fired on 6 pages).
(2) **A filter tuned to last session's finding will reject this session's.** The blind-spot probe v1 required two English words, which is exactly what S177's real finds (`>Auto-detect<`, `<h3>English</h3>`) are not. It returned 10 hits, all correct-by-design, and would have been reported as "clean" — a vacuous pass in the S188 sense. Re-read what the previous run actually found and check your filter would still catch it.
(3) **A dotted key prefix is usually assigned to a variable before it is used.** Anchoring the wildcard scan on `I18n.t('prefix.' + x)` missed `const k = 'fontmaker.glyphname.' + slug` and with it whole families, which is most of the difference between the 1082 and 677 readings. Match any dotted literal ending in a dot that is concatenated with anything, wherever it appears.
(4) **"Add it to `applyI18n`" is not always available.** The obvious fix for the flash-cards pills was to re-run `updateVowelPositionUI` on language change — but it calls `vowelPositions.add(mode)`, and CLAUDE.md requires `applyI18n` to be read-only. Binding each element with `data-i18n` gets the same re-translation through `applyStaticI18n` with no rule broken, and it is the same idiom the suite already uses at `flash_cards.html` `timerShowLabel`. **Grep for an existing idiom before inventing a treatment** — that grep is what supplied the fix shape for all three wiring fixes.
(5) **An element-resolution heuristic needs a confirmation step before anything it says is logged.** Resolving "which element does this setter write to?" by nearest preceding `getElementById` mis-attributed 2 of 4 clobber candidates to an unrelated aria binding on `#wsTitle`. They were dismissible on independent grounds (printed output), which is luck, not method.

**Method notes new in S191 — three separate probes produced a confident WRONG result, and the pass's whole value came from disbelieving them:**
(1) **`getComputedStyle().outlineWidth` cannot detect a focus ring.** Chromium computes its own UA `:focus-visible` ring as `outline: <color> auto 0px` — width **zero** — so a style-read check reported **35 of 64 tab stops as having no focus indicator**. A screenshot pixel-diff (blur → shoot → keyboard-focus → shoot → compare hashes) refuted it **20/20 in both themes**. Any future C run must use pixels, not styles, for this dimension.
(2) **An opaque `rgb()` has no alpha component, so `alpha > 0` is `false` for it.** The fix-verification harness guarded background compositing with `own[3] > 0` and therefore measured every *solid*-backgrounded element against its ancestor's plate instead of its own fill — reporting the gold Copy All button at **1:1** immediately after a fix that was actually correct. The guard must be `alpha === undefined || alpha > 0`. Note this bug is invisible on transparent elements, which is exactly where such a probe is usually pointed.
(3) **A top-level `let` in a non-module inline script is NOT a `window` property.** `window.currentVerses = …` created a shadowed twin: the harness cheerfully reported the reading as injected while `.tt-word` count was **0**, and three probe runs measured an empty page. Bare assignment is the only way into the global lexical environment — and the helper now **throws on a zero-word receipt** rather than letting a silent no-op look like a clean tool.
(4) **`page.click()` focuses the button, so a `contains(activeElement)` rule is not a keyboard-only rule.** The first draft of the dock-focus fix kept the dock visible after *every* mouse Clear too, trading a keyboard fix for a mouse regression — and the code comment asserting "a mouse user never sees an empty dock" was false when written. `:focus-visible` is the platform's own answer to "did they use the keyboard"; gate on it. **The general form: when a fix's justification contains the word "keyboard", the probe must include a mouse arm that can fail.**
(5) **The theme that is broken is the one you were not looking at.** The dock's 1:1 failure existed only in **light** mode because `var(--text)` inverts; dark measured fine throughout. A both-themes contrast probe is not thoroughness here, it is the minimum that can find the class at all.
(6) **A close-out receipt from `git log` is worthless in this container until `git fetch --unshallow` runs.** The clone arrives shallow; `update-sitemap.mjs` correctly refused (the S188 guard doing its job for real this session), and the drift check's `origin/main` position was likewise stale until an explicit fetch — a combined `git fetch origin main <branch>` **aborts entirely** if either ref is missing, silently leaving `origin/main` un-updated and making it look as though nothing had landed.

**Method notes new in S190 — four of the six produced a confident WRONG result first:**
(1) **A cross-tag "parity" assertion has to encode the property the site guarantees, not the one the word implies.** Asserting `og:title === twitter:title` failed **9 of 11 correct pages**: every tool page deliberately ships a shorter, brand-suffixed X variant. The real contract is og-set-complete + `og:url === canonical` + `twitter:image === og:image` + `twitter:card` large — and `twitter:title`/`description`/`image` are *optional* because they fall back to og, while `twitter:card` is not because it has no fallback. Before logging a consistency finding, ask what the spec makes required.
(2) **A diff window that starts after the fix reports the fix as the bug.** `404.html` came back as "missing canonical → FAIL" because my window began at L's last-run boundary and the canonical had been removed one commit earlier (S175, 1c567d4). When a checker contradicts a ledgered DONE entry, check the commit history for that file before believing the checker.
(3) **When structured data makes a claim, find its VISIBLE twin before fixing either.** All 3 JSON-LD findings had an on-page FAQ counterpart carrying the *identical* stale wording in EN and HE. The tempting fix — edit the JSON-LD — would have created a JSON-LD↔visible mismatch that did not previously exist, which is worse than the staleness. The page had fallen behind the tool; both halves had to move.
(4) **Two sibling elements built from one template share a parent, so `parentElement.querySelector('.x')` is not "my .x".** `#letterDrop` and `#combinedDrop` both live in the panel wrapper, so the probe read the *combined* allowed-note twice and failed two correct sites. Use `nextElementSibling` for a known adjacency — and add an assertion that the two readings are *different elements*, so the fault cannot recur silently.
(5) **A constant copied from a neighbour inherits the neighbour's latent bug.** The dock's clearance over the sticky karaoke bar started as `bottom:96px`, lifted from `.tt-hint-toast`. Measuring showed the bar is **77px at 1280, 107px at 800, 167px at 420** — it wraps — so the overlap landed at exactly the width a classroom tablet uses. A `ResizeObserver` publishing the live height fixed it in 6 lines, and the measurement also exposed that `.tt-hint-toast` has the same bug (logged, not bundled). If you copy a magic number, measure the thing it was measuring.
(6) **"No new localStorage key" is the wrong assertion for "adds no state".** Entering the copy bar makes the page persist its own `copyInclude*` toggles, so a new key appears for reasons unrelated to the feature — the check failed correct code. The property a derived-state feature must have is that no **field of its own** is persisted; assert on the settings-blob keys, not on the key list. (Its companion mistake: `settings.hebFontSize = 31; saveSettings()` read back `1.5`, because save is debounced — await it, or assert on a field you actually control.)

**Method notes new in S189 — five of the seven produced a confident WRONG result first:**
(1) **A candidate found by calling the loader directly is not a finding.** Font Maker's "permanently stuck engine status" evaporated the moment I read *who calls* `ensurePyodide` — all three foreground callers do `status('')` plus an error modal. This is S187 note (6) recurring, which means it is not a one-off: **before logging, execute the path a user reaches, not the function the bug lives in.**
(2) **A harness that measures an in-flight window must PROVE it sampled inside it.** A `waitForFunction` on `#ttParshaSelect option` (no direct `option` children) burned the whole 2.6 s window before the first sample, and torah_trainer read as showing no busy text at all. Every arm now prints its window bounds and its in-window sample count; a zero there is a harness failure, not a clean tool.
(3) **`stallHits === 0` must be a hard failure, never a clean result.** Two surfaces reported "no busy text" because the trigger functions I invented (`setMode`, `setBlendMode`, `setCardMode`) do not exist. Grep the setter before calling it — and assert the resource under test was actually requested.
(4) **A fix that passes in English and fails in Hebrew is a timing signal, not a flake.** The HE locale fetch resolves later, so a re-render lands *after* the failure handler — which is exactly what exposed that the dictionary's real cause was the render path, not the catch. The English-only run would have shipped a fix that works in one language.
(5) **Drive the real UI path, not the loader.** Calling `ensureRealWords()` directly left `#rwPoolInfo` hidden, so the "0 matching words" claim was unproven until the toggle was ticked with a real `change` event. A finding about what a teacher SEES needs the element visible in the receipt.
(6) **A negative control must be able to fail for the right reason.** "The tree renders once loaded" with `kids > 5` failed correct code — the emoji tree has exactly 2 visible categories. Assert the shape the code actually produces; an exact count beats an invented threshold.
(7) **When the fix is "tell the truth about a load", check whether the first write precedes the fetch.** Every generator trigger path painted the caption before `ensureRealWords()` was called, so a correct guard never fired. Fixing it inside the loader covered all four call sites at once — cheaper and safer than patching the one path the test happened to exercise.

**Method notes new in S188 — three of the four produced a confident WRONG result first:**
(1) **`content=` is not a path.** A ref-integrity scan that treats `href|src|content` alike reports meta descriptions and whole CSP strings as broken links: **213 "broken refs", 210 of them `content=`.** Scan `href`/`src`, plus only the meta tags that genuinely hold a URL (og:image, og:url, twitter:*). And keep `url(...)` inside `<style>` regions — in JS it matches `new URL(req.url)`.
(2) **A vacuous pass is worse than a failure.** The apple-startup check matched `apple-touch-startup-image"\s+href=`, but `media=` comes first — so it found **0 tags** and then cheerfully reported "block identical across all 12 production pages", because comparing nothing to nothing succeeds. Any cross-file identity assertion needs a non-zero-count assertion beside it.
(3) **One open-marker string can head two different shared blocks** (app-toast CSS vs JS, folder-tree CSS vs JS), **and the end marker contains the open marker.** A naive `indexOf(open)` measures the wrong block and reports phantom "OPEN with no close" lines. Walk every occurrence and label each block by *which* close marker it reaches.
(4) **LOADED vs LINKED is the whole distinction for a dependency audit.** Counting every `https://` host in the HTML made `resources.html`'s link directory look like 82 third-party dependencies; restricting to actual loads (script/link/iframe/media/@font-face/fetch/Worker) gives **11**. A licence audit that can't tell a `<script src>` from an `<a href>` is measuring nothing.
(5) **`URLSearchParams` encodes spaces as `+`, and `decodeURIComponent` does NOT undo that.** A route fixture matching a version title with `decodeURIComponent(url).includes(title)` silently never matched, served the wrong body to the *recovery* fetch, and reported the fix as broken — 8 failing assertions against correct code. Normalise `+`→`%20` before decoding a query value.
(6) **Seed the setting the page actually reads.** A harness seeding `layout:'stacked'` (no such value; the real keys are `sidebyside`/`interlinear`/`biblepage`) silently rendered a different layout whose English cells use a different class, so the assertion measured `0` rows that were never supposed to exist. Grep the setter before seeding the setting.
(7) **Verify a fix against a PRE-FIX copy of the page, served side by side.** `git show HEAD:page.html > _tmp.html` in the repo root (deleted before committing) makes the before/after a single harness run with identical fixtures — the only way to be sure the fix, and not the fixture, is what changed the result.
(8) **A standing rule is not a fix.** S175 found the shallow-clone date artifact, corrected the data, and wrote a standing rule; S187 broke it 13 sessions later. When the remedy for a silently-wrong artifact is "remember to do X first", the real fix is to make the tool refuse — and prove it with a counterfactual (unguarded, a `--depth 1` clone collapses all 11 sitemap dates to one).

**Method notes new in S187 — every one of these produced a confident WRONG result first:**
(1) **A receipt has to assert the thing happened, not that the page still exists.** Three interaction arms reported "0 channel hits" while doing nothing at all: the flash-cards drill never started (guessed `#cardCount`/`.card` — the real state is `cardCount`/`flipCard()`/`currentCards`), the dictionary searched an unloaded corpus (0 results until a `waitForFunction` on `ALL_WORDS`), and the generator's dark toggle is `#darkToggle`, not `#darkBtn`. This is S172's lesson recurring verbatim in the same pass — write the receipt as an assertion with a number in it.
(2) **A cached `boundingBox()` is a stale coordinate at narrow widths.** At 800px the FM layout stacks, so clicking a control in the left panel scrolled the stage to `y = −479` and every subsequent stroke landed off-canvas: 14 gestures → **0 strokes**, reported as "0 channel hits". Re-read the box (and `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded`) **per gesture**, and treat a zero-effect count as a harness failure, not a clean result.
(3) **`addInitScript` re-runs on every navigation.** Seeding a localStorage flag there and then reloading to check an erase re-writes the flag *after* the erase — which read exactly like "pwa.js re-sets the dismissal on load", a finding that does not exist. Seed with `page.evaluate` after the first load when the test involves a reload.
(4) **Chromium collapses repeated identical console messages, so a console count is a LOWER bound.** The negative-width rect printed **1** line for **4** real assignments. Wrapping the `innerHTML` setter gave both the true frequency and a JS stack naming the function — worth doing whenever the console says "1" and the code path looks like it should fire on every render.
(5) **Computed style is not visible damage.** 29 elements computing `direction:rtl` produced **0** visually reordered strings, because bidi keeps a single uninterrupted LTR run in logical order. Measure glyph positions (`Range.getBoundingClientRect()` per character, EN vs HE) before logging an RTL hit — the amended detection rule is now in Pattern health.
(6) **Ground a candidate's reachability, not just its literal.** The FM "no ink detected" literal was real, but unreachable the way the candidate implied: entering Trace never runs that line (three builders emit `''`); only `doRetrace()` — a threshold drag — does. Verifying the state the candidate *describes* rather than the state the code *reaches* would have "passed" without ever executing the fix.
(7) **A media `abort` is not a media failure.** Confirming S183: fulfil with a **502** to get a real `MediaError`; an abort leaves the element stalled and silently skips the error path you are trying to test.

**⚑ PASS D IS NOW COMPLETE — do NOT run it again next session.** S185 finished all three surfaces S184 never reached (copy+bulk-select scaling, karaoke runtime, session hygiene) and every one came back clean, with the numbers recorded in the rotation row above. D has now had two consecutive sessions; **it is the freshest pass in the rotation and should not be picked again for a long while.** The harness that made it cheap is described well enough in the S184/S185 rotation rows to rebuild from scratch — S185 did exactly that, which is the evidence that writing the description down works.

**Method notes new in S185 — every one of these produced a confident WRONG result first:**
(1) **Measure the thing at the moment it is under test, not before.** A "short reading vs full reading" comparison was invalid because the chant button re-rendered the full parsha AFTER the 12-verse measurement was taken — the receipt (`got`) and the run disagreed and nothing said so. Fix: assert the live verse/word/px count **at playback time**, and cap the FIXTURE rather than slicing state in-page.
(2) **A cheap stub makes a fast result meaningless in one direction.** The transliteration ESM is served as a local regex stub (esm.sh returns 403 through this container's proxy), so every translit-on timing is a LOWER bound, not a measurement. State the direction of the bias when a stub is in the loop.
(3) **A multi-part candidate goes stale in parts.** The last open P2 named three diagnostics; two had already been fixed by S182 and S183, neither of which traced back to the umbrella entry. Re-ground each part before scheduling a multi-part item — and when closing one part, tick the umbrella.
(4) **A drafted one-line fix in a candidate is a hypothesis, not a patch.** The S181 RTL candidate's proposed rule would have pinned the COLUMN, flipping translated Hebrew `<h3>` headers as a side effect. Check what else inherits from the element you are about to pin.
(5) **Adding a pre-ready builder to `applyI18n` can install a leak.** If that builder also registers a listener on a target that survives re-render, every language switch adds another. Name the handler; then count (5x applyI18n -> 0 added).
(6) **Subagent fan-out is still broken in this container** (the S174 fault): 3 workflow agents, ~237k tokens, 0 findings — every tool call came back with its parameters stripped. Run discovery inline.

**Method notes new in S184 — both would have produced a confident wrong answer:**
(1) **A stub that is more generous than the real API hides the bug you are testing for.** The Sefaria fixture returned an English version on every request; the real API returns one only when `version=english|…` is asked for. Two checks PASSED on unfixed code before that was corrected.
(2) **Assert the property the code actually guarantees, not the one that sounds right.** A te'amim check that included sof pasuk (U+05C3) and paseq (U+05C0) failed a correct implementation — `TEAMIM_RE` is U+0591–U+05AF, and the punctuation is kept on purpose.
(3) **A background workflow does not survive a session suspend.** Its agents die silently: the journal simply stops growing and no notification ever arrives. Check agent-file mtimes and `ps` for browser processes before waiting on one — and re-measure by hand anything whose verify phase never ran.
(4) **An optimisation can regress the common case.** The first `wlNamesForIds` always read the store, which would have added a parse for every teacher who selects no word lists — the case the old code handled for free. Check the empty/zero input before claiming a win.



**Variety was deliberately restored.** S182 spent 5/5 in one tool under an explicit cap override; S183 touched seven surfaces (font-maker, index, generator, flash-cards, dashboard, torah-trainer once each, dictionary twice) with **no override needed**. Tool coverage is now the most even it has been in months — every tool except trope-tutor was touched today. **trope-tutor is now the stalest tool at S170**, and it is also an unswept carrier of the pattern S183 registered (its `#tuAudio` engine plays the same PocketTorah MP3s), so a trope-tutor session would serve two purposes.

**The P2 shelf is nearly bare.** What remains is mostly P3 clusters: the torah_trainer Pass-G print P3s (RTL English column — one line, affects screen AND print; header/legend on sheet 1 only; the nikkud underline's 2px that never applies because a shorthand resets `text-decoration-thickness`; the trope legend printing as a 35% tint of solid marks, which collapses to grey on a photocopy), the S180 Pass-H copy/bulk and audio-ergonomics clusters, and the dictionary's 15-finding touch-target haul. **Two open P2s remain:** torah_trainer's copy bar silently copying nothing with "Include translation" on, and the bulk-copy preview not refreshing when te'amim/nikkud are hidden — both in the same copy workflow, so they are one sensible session.

**Three patterns are registered but swept on ONE page each** (S182's two + S183's one), and the sibling surfaces are named in each sweep-status entry. That is a Pass A waiting to happen: *print-trailing-dead-space* (generator + flash cards both print heavily), *lazily-loaded-dependency-renders-an-empty-shell*, and *silent-external-media-failure* (trope tutor's clip engine, the dashboard's YouTube embed). **Note the S183 finding that closes one of those leads early:** the generator, flash cards and dictionary all pass a real corpus `defaultTranslit` into `HebrewTransliterator.apply()`, so they degrade to real text rather than an empty shell — torah_trainer was the only carrier without a fallback. Verified by inspection, not measurement.

**Method notes carried:** all prior notes stand. New in S183 — every one of these produced a convincing FALSE result before being caught:
(1) **A `preload="none"` media element fetches nothing when you assign `src`.** No request, no error, no failure path — the element looks healthy. Drive `play()`.
(2) **`route.abort()` on a media resource leaves the element STALLED, not errored.** Fulfil with an error response instead; that is also the more realistic model of a blocking school proxy.
(3) **A `display:none` ancestor makes Chromium drop a node from the AX tree entirely**, so an accessible-name probe returns null — indistinguishable from a genuinely missing label. Expand every collapsed panel before probing anything in a drawer.
(4) **Shell quoting mangles regexes in `node -e`.** A broken route pattern silently falls through to the harness's abort-everything catch-all and manufactures the exact failure the test is trying to rule out. Write probes to a file.
(5) **Assert the property the pattern actually requires.** A WCAG bypass link belongs immediately before the repeated block, not near the page top; the first assertion ("reachable within 12 tabs") failed a correct implementation.
(6) **A naive `grep -c 'alert('` counts your own comments.** Exclude comment lines before quoting a census as a trend number.
